ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u4	Mr. President! Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! Ladies and gentlemen of the Ministers! High Izbo! Your Excellency! Dear members of the Diplomat Corps! Ladies and gentlemen! Today I have the honour to present for the first time as Minister of Foreign Affairs in the new, reconstructed office of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki to the Sejm, gathered guests and citizens of the Republic of Poland information on the tasks of Polish foreign policy in the year two thousand and eighteen. [[Voice from the room: 2,000 and 18th.]] It is an honor for me to present the High House's foreign policy programme for the year in which Poland has its 100th anniversary of regaining independence. Thank you for the presence of the President, who represents the majesty of the Republic of Poland in the field of foreign policy. [[Applause]] In the field of foreign policy, in accordance with the provisions of the Constitution, he shall cooperate with the President of the Council of Ministers and the Minister of Foreign Affairs. Close cooperation between the President and the Government, mutual support in the service of the Republic strengthens Poland's position and is a prerequisite for the effectiveness of Polish foreign policy. High Seym! According to Art. 4 of the Constitution, the sovereignty of the Republic of Poland belongs to the nation. Foreign policy is therefore shaped according to citizens' wishes. The Minister of Foreign Affairs, like any other member of the Council of Ministers, shall be accountable to the Sejm. It is the Sejm who controls the ministers, making each of them responsible for the affairs of the Republic, which he entrusts to him, on behalf of the sovereign, or nation, by giving the Council of Ministers a vote of confidence. At the same time, the Sejm itself, as a gathering of representatives of the nation, makes sovereign decisions, some of which concern foreign policy, creating the conditions resulting from these decisions for further activities of diplomacy. In direct elections, citizens also elect the President of the Republic of Poland, who co-creates foreign policy. Therefore, the content of the Constitution is based on the main principle of Polish foreign policy - the principle of subjectivity reflected in the slogan: nothing about us without us. [[Applause]] It is abstinence to accept as binding any decision taken on our subject without our participation. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The obvious reason of the state of Poland is to guarantee national independence and sovereign statehood. [[Applause]] It is also to ensure the civilizational development of Poland in the subjective relations within the international community. This leads me to put four also with regard to the challenges, goals and nature of Polish foreign policy. The first thesis: Poland's international position is due to its strong position in Europe. The source of the attractiveness of Poland and its ability to influence its decision-making processes effectively, whether as an ally within NATO or as a partner in the structures of the European Union, is its ability to articulate the interests of the countries of our region and act as their attorney. This can be done in full respect of the political subjectivity of our neighbourhood partners. In Poland as the largest country in the region lies the main burden of promoting and defending the Polish interests of our regional partners. The second point: The European Union is in crisis in both institutional, axiological and external security. Although the 2008 financial crisis has ended economically, its effects The political and social role of the Union in the future of the European project continues to have an impact on the nature of relations between Member States. The crisis of democratic procedures in the Member States and the transfer of real decision-making processes to informal bodies lead to a weakening of the Union's position, the rise of euroscepticism, the renationalisation of European policies. The third thesis: the military presence of the United States in Europe and their strong position in NATO are of fundamental importance for the military security of Poland and the whole region. It is in the interest of Poland and Central and Eastern Europe that the United States and the North Atlantic Alliance are permanently involved in this part of the world. Poland is as interested as possible in maintaining strong transatlantic ties. [[We need to talk to the Secretary of State.]] Their continuous strengthening and development are the fundamental task of the Polish security policy. The fourth thesis: Russia's policy is a threat to building the subjectivity of Poland in international relations. Russia is pursuing a revision of the political order in Europe, which developed after 1989 and brought Poland back to independence. The instruments for achieving this goal are the destabilization of many regions in the closer and further vicinity of Poland, action to strengthen political divisions within and between countries, breaking down transatlantic unity and deepening divisions within the European Union. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! After drawing up the general conditions of Polish foreign policy, I would like to move on to specific issues. First of all, I will discuss what we are doing to strengthen our security in an unstable international environment and our relations with our main ally: the United States. I will then present our vision of European policy and the state of play with our main European partners. I will devote another part of my speech to regional cooperation. I will then discuss the goals of Polish diplomacy in cooperation with Polonia and development cooperation, as well as recent changes in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. High Izbo! National security of the Republic of Poland is the highest priority of Polish foreign policy. The old slogan of every reasonable diplomacy: First of all, security is also our signpost. National security of the Republic of Poland, in addition to the obvious basis in the defence system of the Polish state, is founded on the strength and coherence of the North Atlantic Alliance. The government, together with the President, has been working effectively since autumn 2015 to strengthen NATO's military presence in the region. In cooperation with Romania, we have succeeded in building solidarity between the states, which was reflected in the creation of the Bucharest Nine. The postulates put forward by this group of countries in our region for the military reinforcement of the eastern NATO flank were adopted at the Warsaw Summit in 2016 and implemented last year. Poland hosts allied forces from the United States, the United Kingdom, Romania and Croatia in its territory. Allow me to express my gratitude to our allies for the solidarity shown to Poland in this way. [[Applause]] Poland as one of several countries of the Alliance has spent 2% on defence for several years GDP. True, strong economic growth and a change in the way of counting led to a decrease of 0.01% in this share last year. However, this does not mean moving away from the principle that the size of defence expenditure depends on the credibility of NATO's deterrent and defence policy. The Polish Army participates in NATO-Wskie missions in Latvia and Romania. According to the allied schedule, the Polish aviation contingent serves in the Baltic States, guarding the sky over Lithuania, Latvia and Estonia. Cooperation is ongoing with Germany and Denmark within the Multinational North-East Corps with headquarters in Szczecin and cooperation with Lithuania and Ukraine within the Polish-Lithuanian-Ukrainian Brigade activated in 2016. We have become a provider of common security, not just to the recipient. Poland will seek to increase NATO's military presence and infrastructure in the region, recognising that only credible, really strong deterrents can provide us with peace and security. The aim of Polish diplomacy is to increase the efficiency of the Alliance's operation, especially in the field of collective defence. We will strive to provide support forces, strengthen ally mobility, realistic planning and speed up decision-making processes. Our proposals in this area are now subject to alliance agreements. Poland is in favour of the North Atlantic Alliance maintaining open door policy. We want to develop cooperation with NATO partner countries, especially with its immediate environment: Finland, Sweden, Ukraine, Moldova and Georgia, with which we have a far-reaching convergence of views on the nature of threats to international security. High Izbo! The details of military cooperation both within the North Atlantic Alliance and within the European Union are the responsibility of the Ministry of National Defence. In the political dimension of Polish military activity, I would just like to stress that the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland guard the security of Poland and build the credibility of our country as a solid ally in missions outside NATO and European Union Member States. In 2017, the Polish Army took part in 13 foreign missions: seven NATO-Warsaws in Afghanistan, Kosovo, Latvia, Romania and the Baltic States, training and training in Ukraine and the command mission of a permanent staff team, three EU-led in Georgia, Bosnia and Herzegovina and the Central African Republic, two coalitions in Kuwait and Iraq, and one bilateral training team in Ukraine. Since the beginning of this year the mandate of the Polish quota in Romania also covers Bulgaria. In February of this year, the Polish Army's contingent joined the EU Mission in the Mediterranean since 2015. In addition, Polish officers and officers participate in EU missions in Ukraine, Moldova and maritime operations off the Somali coast. I would like to thank the soldiers of the Polish Army, Polish officers and civil servants for their sacrificial service to their country outside the country. [[Applause]] This commitment builds the ally credibility of Poland in the bosom of NATO and the European Union. Thank you again. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Our goal is to further deepen our security ties with the United States. We will develop both bilateral cooperation and cooperation in multilateral fora, primarily in NATO. We are opposed to any step that provokes transatlantic divisions. We will strive for a more permanent anchoring of elements of the U.S. armed forces in the territory of Poland. This political line on the whole Eastern NATO flank is shared by our allies from the Bucharest Nine. The American base in Redzików is an important element in defending NATO territory against a potential attack. We note technical delays in its construction, which the American side signalled as early as mid-January this year. We look forward to further cooperation and detailed information on this issue. The United States participates in the modernization of the Polish Armed Forces. Our partnership increasingly embraces the economic dimension as well as a promising area of innovation and the development of high technologies. We hope that the Polish-American Economic Summit planned for April will give a greater impetus to our cooperation in this field. Cooperation on the release of the Central European natural gas market depends on the dependence on Russian supplies is of strategic importance. 2018 will be the first year of implementation of the 5-year contract for the supply of American gas. [[Applause]] Ladies and gentlemen! Our membership of the Security Council The UN is a greater influence and greater responsibility for global problems. We will focus on strengthening the principles of international law, preventing conflicts and tackling new threats to peace and security. Poland is in the position of fully respecting the fundamental principles of international law, the integrity of borders, respect for sovereignty, respect for human rights and the renunciation of military force. On the resolution of the conflict in Syria, we support the Geneva Declaration and the negotiations under the auspices of the United Nations. We will support conflict resolution in the Democratic Republic of Congo and South Sudan. As Chairman of the Sanctions Committees, we pay particular attention to the situation in Sudan and South Sudan. As a member of the Security Council, we demand the cessation of Russia's destabilising actions towards Ukraine and we stress the need for a peaceful settlement of the Donbas conflict. We attach great importance to humanitarian issues, including the protection of the rights of religious communities. In inaugurating Poland's membership of the Security Council in January this year, President Andrzej Duda took an active part in the debate on the non-plorification of weapons of mass destruction. I participated in the discussions on security in Afghanistan and Central Asia and on the role of the United Nations Charter in ensuring peace and security. In May, our country will take the rotating Presidency of the Council, during which the President will hold a debate on strengthening confidence in international law in the modern world. Poland will chair the preparatory committee of the review conference of the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons Agreement, to be held in 2020, on the 50th anniversary of the entry into force of the Agreement. [[Euro!]] The fundamental issue for the effectiveness of the European Union is the ability to achieve a genuine democratic mandate to act. [[Applause]] A strong Union is a Union with the support of its states and nations. An efficient Union is one that has a democratic mandate to use the resources generated by the citizens of the Member States to achieve democratically defined objectives. The Union's first task is therefore to gain the political support of its citizens for its actions. Such agreement in the form of a real mandate can only be obtained at the level of national Parliaments. Further deepening of European integration is only possible to the extent accepted by the participating countries and nations. [[Applause]] I would also like to say that the European Commission is not a supergovernment, and the European Parliament is not a superparliament empowered to instruct national governments and parliaments. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union states: the Union shall act only within the limits of the powers conferred on it by the Member States and any competence not conferred on the Union by the Treaties shall belong to the Member States. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the hall: Sacred words.]] [[And Member States are states of law.]] High Izbo! The deficit of democracy in the European Union is not a rhetorical figure, but a reality with deep political consequences. The Republic of Poland takes the view that the European Union can only be strong by real support to its citizens, who are capable of giving democratic power to their elected governments to rule. Citizens want their voice to matter. The growing wave of opposition to integration on the European continent is not the cause of the Union's democratic weakness, but is the result of it. [[Applause]] Nothing harms the idea of European integration so much as the actual inequality of its Member States towards law, the application of double standards and the departure of the European Commission from the role of objective guardian of the Treaties [[Applause]] to the functions of instruments in the hands of certain countries. Poland is an old democracy with one of the longest parliamentary traditions in Europe. [[She needs to be respected.]] This year we celebrate the 550th anniversary of Polish parliamentarianism. One of the creators of the Constitution on 3 May, Hugo Kołłątaj, wrote: "It is wrong to accuse the nations that they are being raised into government, because they are born into fornication. After more than two centuries, this quote has not lost its relevance and shows the true cause of opposition to European integration in many countries. We believe in the common sense of voters and reject the right of anyone to teach their fellow citizens what to believe. [[Applause]] [[Bravo!]] [[Except for Sundays off.]] High Izbo! We are determined opponents of the European Union of two speeds. A clear result of the creation of a core of Europe or a first-speed Europe would be the transfer of any real decision-making process from the Treaty institutions of the European Union, in which all Member States are represented, to the newly created institutions of the euro area and informal bodies. This would lead to the marginalisation of Central Europe and other regions: Scandinavia, the southern countries of the euro area, which are struggling with the effects of the financial crisis. Setting up a directorate of selected powers would be contrary to fundamental European principles. An efficient, functional democracy requires the rejection of protectionism. Its expression is the draft directive on posted workers. We cannot accept the rules of the game within which our competitive advantages should be crossed by the rules imposed by stronger partners... [[Voice from the gym: We lost.]] ...and their rules, their competitive advantages should be maintained. We believe in an integrated European single market and four freedoms - I stress - all four freedoms: the movement of goods, capital, services and people, and not just some of them. [[Applause]] We are in favour of a social market economy based on free competition and economic freedoms. Protectionism restricts competitiveness and therefore economic growth, resulting in a decline in the importance of force and therefore the importance of the European Union in the world. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The Structural Funds are part of a mechanism that makes the common European market beneficial to all its players. They compensate weaker partners paid by a more economically developed country in exchange for the abolition of protectionist duties and quotas and for opening less competitive economies to free competition from stronger ones. Hence, funds are not a charitable donation to the poorer. Most of these funds return to net payers. The Polish and other countries of our region remain open to all European partners. We therefore expect the other party to fulfil its commitments until we have reached the level of development of the countries of the old Union. Then, naturally, we will become net contributors, also benefiting from it. [[Applause]] We are in favour of maintaining a high level of funding for the Union's cohesion policy in the future budgetary perspective for the period 2021 to 2027. Poland is a leader in the efficient and transparent use of EU funds. [[Voice from the room: Was.]] We are therefore aware that the measures will decrease. [[Because of you.]] The United Kingdom, which was a net payer, is from the Union. Some Polish regions have achieved such a level of income that they will lose the right to funds from structural policy. At the same time, we are in favour of an ambitious budget, filling the gaps by giving up discounts and increasing contributions. [[Applause]] We declare the willingness to increase the contribution paid by Poland accordingly. [[Right after we got back from Washington.]] High Izbo! We want to be involved in solving the migration crisis. However, we are, as we stress, opposed to imposing a system of mandatory reception quotas for refugees. Experience so far shows that they are going to those countries that are able to provide them with better living conditions. Enhanced cooperation in defence and security - PESCO addresses three important issues: the development of the national arms industry, the structure of the national defence budget and the effectiveness and speed of cross-border transfer of allied troops. The European Defence Fund aims to fund research into new defence technologies and to support Member States in developing combat capabilities. This will have an obvious impact on the structures of the Union's national military budgets and their arms industries. We are in favour of equal opportunities for access to the defence industry fund of all Member States to ensure that innovation and jobs are increased in each Member State. We want to avoid the situation where the largest countries with a large defence industry benefit most from the Commission's proposals, while smaller countries will be at risk of reducing their potential. It is also necessary to develop those technologies and capabilities which are essential for the defence of the Union's own territory. We strongly support the principle of non-repairing the North Atlantic Alliance's activities and expenditure. Speaking of strengthening Europe's defence capabilities, we must not forget our British allies. We stress that the European Union needs Britain also after it leaves the organisation. [[Applause]] The relationship between the two parties should be as close as possible, but based on the balance of rights and obligations. At the initiative of President Andrzej Duda, the Warsaw Tri-Morch Summit in 2017 with the participation of the President of the United States, Donald Trump, gave this initiative a new dynamic. [[Applause]] The aim of the initiative is to translate into concrete infrastructure rules on the single market. The exchange of goods, services and mobility of workers remain far below the existing potential in our region. The barrier is the lack of road, rail and energy infrastructure in the north-south band. We look forward to strengthening and concreteising cooperation, among others. in the form of the creation of the Tri-marine Fund, from which infrastructure projects can be financed. We value the practice of trilateral Polish-Romanian-Turkish consultations in the field of foreign and security policy. The country will hold the Presidency of the European Union next year. We also appreciate Turkey's sensitive role as an important member of the North Atlantic Alliance. Cooperation with our neighbours in the north is developing, especially in security policy, arms cooperation, the European agenda and sectoral cooperation. Opening up to Scandinavia: Denmark, Norway, Sweden and Finland would also be a natural direction for developing the infrastructure investments of the Tri-marine. We value dialogue in a format that connects Central Europe and Scandinavia. With the Baltic States: Lithuania, Latvia, Estonia, we have similar perceptions of the nature of threats, convergence of interests with regard to the European Union, common energy and infrastructure interests. The meeting of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki with the leaders of the Baltic states recently confirmed the solidarity of our countries. Ladies and gentlemen! Mr Fellowships, Mr Fellow Members, Eastern policy is one of the most important dimensions of the foreign policy of the Republic of Poland. Both the current dynamics of the situation in the east, the scale of challenges coming from this direction for the security of Poland and Europe, as well as our historical experiences, will determine this. It is in Poland's interest that the peoples of its eastern neighbourhood enjoy independence and security so that, in accordance with the spirit and letter of the Charter of the United Nations and the principles of the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe, their right to sovereignly choose the path of development, political system and alliances are respected. Those of them who decide on their pro-European and pro-Western options can count on Poland's persistent help to achieve their goal. [[Applause]] We welcomed the results of last year's Eastern Partnership Summit in Brussels. We will continue to support our partners, taking advantage of the opportunities offered by the European Union, NATO and on the basis of bilateral relations. High Seym! In the interest of Poland and Europe there are pragmatic relations with the Russian Federation. Polish diplomacy is working towards a joint and consistent NATO and European Union policy towards Russia. Our consistent position should take into account the Russian behaviour towards both the Union and NATO as well as the individual Member States, our strategic partners and Russia's compliance with international law. We consider it necessary to maintain a political dialogue with Russia. Its essence, however, cannot be to ignore its current aggressive policy towards the West. Russia has violated many solemnly adopted treaties in recent years. I hope... This can at the same time be a very big barrier to the development of our homelands and the Union itself, because only an indivisible, lasting Union and unity guarantee the development and resolution of problems for the benefit of our citizens, and we have a lot of these problems in the European Union: security, position in global trade, protection of our common social model, global competitiveness. None of these problems can be solved by dividing. A divided Union is a smaller scale of impact on the environment. I would like to stress very strongly that solidarity and mutual responsibility of the Member States must be the basis for our joint action in the European Union. This is essential for the success of the integration process across Europe. Another thing: there should be no place in the Union to dominate the strongest players. We believe that all Member States are equal and should equally be taken into account in political decisions. The shape and future of the Union should be decided by all Member States in agreement and consensus, taking into account the well-being of our citizens. Of course, we believe that this should be under the aegis - including the whole reform of the European Union - of the European Council. The second very important thing is that national Parliaments have a greater role to play. If we want to restore citizens' confidence in the Union, to the European project, if we want to strengthen democratic legitimacy, then we must strengthen national oversight of legislative and political processes and thus strengthen the role of national parliaments. We also talk about this very often in our committee forums in the European Union, and here it is in a kind of consensus. It seems that the citizens of the European Union have more confidence in their own parliaments and are looking at the foundations of democratic legitimacy there, and any ideas for strengthening the European Parliament will certainly not resolve the doubts about the democratic deficit. Here we could also talk about the yellow card, the red card, the green card, the European Commission's decision-making even against citizens' will and the failure of national parliaments. I believe that the European Commission must absolutely not ignore the votes of national parliaments. I would therefore like to stress that the Law and Justice are in favour of the reform of the Union, politically and institutionally in favour of the central role of the governments of the Member States, including the leading role of the European Council, and the value of national parliaments. In economic terms, however, we are the leader in the completion of the single market, including the digital market, creating coalitions against protectionist tendencies. On the external side, we are in favour of restoring the importance of enlargement policy and a strategic approach to the transatlantic alliance, an increased Union activity in both sides of the neighbourhood. We are opposed to all populist, protectionist actions that are in fact a threat to the common market. Tomorrow, as a committee, we are putting forward a resolution initiative on cohesion policy, which is an important element of the common market. In general, the EU budget cannot be modified without modifying other market elements, which is why we stress above all that cohesion policy, the common agricultural policy is directly enshrined in the Treaties and we really do not need any scares. That's what we have in the treaties. It also brings tangible benefits to other Member States: it is calculated how many individual countries receive back to their budget with 1 euro. Of course, there will also be a need to fill the budget gap due to the UK's exit from the EU. We propose and agree to those voices which propose an increase in contributions, but, of course, it must also be a proportional increase. Agricultural policy, a very important topic for us. It is important here that the funds of the two pillars of the common agricultural policy are properly distributed and that the rate of direct payments is equal. Poland looks forward to fully aligning direct payments as soon as possible and ensuring a fair common agricultural policy. The last important element that we are still living is migration policy. In the area of migration - Poland emphasizes this from the beginning - the most effective tools are those that reduce the causes of migration. ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u70	Minister! Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In my speech, I wanted to highlight the topic of reparation and compensation of war in Polish-German relations and to draw attention to it. Well, High Izbo, in Polish-German relations, issues of reparation and compensation of war for the Republic of Poland and for individuals after World War II have never been settled effectively and definitively. [[That's why we didn't bring it up at the Angela Merkel meeting.]] The policy of Germany towards Poland can be characterized as a game of silence, statute of limitations and oblivion. The Polish peace treaty, which would end World War II - as was the case after World War I - has not yet been concluded with Poland - in which Germany would settle for material and personal damage. Germany also never accounted for the works of art and cultural goods exported from Poland. This problem in relation to Poland over the years has always been pushed back to the background for various reasons, with different arguments, which is why today this issue returns in a public debate with doubled social and media power. Therefore, a parliamentary team was formed in the Polish Sejm, which analyzes the issue of reparation and compensation after the Second World War over the last 70 years. An analysis of the archive documentation, legal expertise and behaviour of Germany towards other countries affected by World War II is being carried out. Experts are also working on the report on the war losses of the Republic of Poland. During World War II Poland suffered the greatest loss of property and loss of national assets, as well as personal losses from all European countries in relation to the total population. These losses were not only due to war activities, but mainly to the German occupation policy, in particular the deliberate and organised extermination of civilians in occupied areas of the Polish state, as well as the intensive exploitation of Polish society, including forced labour, and the deliberate destruction of property, including the destruction of the capital of the Polish state - Warsaw. During the German occupation more than 6 million Polish citizens lost their lives. The number of Polish citizens in 1939 was 35 million. After the war, this number of citizens decreased by 12 million and amounted to only 23 million. As a result of the war more than 13 million Polish citizens were injured. More than 200,000 were transported to Germany for Germanization purposes. children. The permanent disability was affected by 590,000. persons. A total of 2.5 million people were deported to slave labor, while 2.5 million more were displaced, mainly from eastern Poland or from Pomerania. The losses of the property of the Republic amounted to almost 38% of the state of 1939. Losses of material substance, e.g. Warsaw, 84% , in the Polish industry - 90% , in residential buildings - 72% , in culture and monuments - 90%. The Germans destroyed and robbed 43% cultural heritage of Poland. Polish libraries lost 66% harvesting. Under international law, and in particular the Fourth Hague Convention, the war party which has violated the provisions of the above Rules of Procedure will be liable to compensation if necessary. In addition, he will be responsible for any action by persons belonging to his armed forces. The findings of the Potsdam Conference and Germany's proceedings against other countries affected during the Second World War, consisting in concluding compensation agreements with them and paying compensation, indicate that the German State should compensate the Polish State for the damage associated with the period of the Second World War. It should be stressed that the Polish People's Republic has made numerous attempts to address this problem through the United Nations. And already in 1969 Polish delegates to the United Nations Committee on Human Rights stressed that Polish citizens do not receive compensation until today due to the discriminatory legislation of the Federal Republic of Germany and the Federal Republic of Germany do not feel obliged to repay this tragic debt towards the Polish people. So this situation shows that already in 1969 Polish delegates - it was the famous Professor Resich, who was a Polish delegate to the UN - pointed to the non-regulation of the issue of compensation for individuals. And today, in 2018, the Minister of Foreign Affairs in the Polish Sejm says that this case should be settled. It has not been regulated for 70 years, ladies and gentlemen, with premeditated and full responsibility, that all these injured people, 13 million people have died or died until today without any compensation from the German state. We need to be fully aware of what we are talking about, ladies and gentlemen. In conclusion, the Polish delegate to the United Nations stressed that the United Nations should conclude the rules on compensation for victims of World War II. Although the rules were set out in the resolutions of the legal committee, Germany has not yet complied with these commitments. In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to stress that an outstanding expert in international law, Prof. Klafkowski, has made it clear that international law does not recognise an institution for the limitation of war crimes and crimes against humanity, nor does it recognise the limitation of compensation for such crimes. It should also be stressed that in the Treaty 2+4 in 1990. Poland has been omitted as a participant in this treaty. The issue of war compensation has not been settled either. The only benefits that were paid were those paid by the Polish-German Reconciliation Foundation, but these benefits concerned only a narrow group of people and were disproportionately low in relation to the potential claims of these people. It should be pointed out that, since the end of World War II, Germany has paid approximately EUR 75 billion to the victims of the Third Reich around the world. However, most of these funds were paid in the Federal Republic of Germany itself, Western Europe, Israel, the United States, and only less than 2% , because 1400 million euro, this amount went to Polish citizens. The amount given by Mr Grumpy in his speech was taken from the German media, which provided false information. It is a pity that, after many months of debate, Members of the Civic Platform refer to the German media and not to the data that result from the data of the Polish-German Reconciliation Foundation. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u160	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to thank all the presidents of the clubs, the Members who spoke in today's debate. We take all these voices very carefully, as a contribution to defining the priorities of foreign policy, how to implement them, and questions as an expression of concern for the citizens of Poland, who make their demands through MEPs. A number of questions have been raised, some of which I responded to during my first speech following the speech of the club presidents. But there are still many specific questions. I would like to respond to some of them today and we will send you some answers as soon as possible. Mr Grzegorz Schetyna - these threads appeared in the statements of other Members, among others. Mr Grzegorz Długo - he asked about competitions in Europe, in the European Union, why Poles do not win. I would like to say that Poles run for elections to the European institutions and are also successful. Let me remind you that in March Ub. Mr Piotr Dmochowski-Lipski was elected as Executive Secretary of the European Satellite Telecommunications Organisation, and in January this year Mr Paweł Pikus was elected member of the Administrative Board of the Agency for. Cooperation of Energy Regulators. The success of our candidates was preceded by intense campaigns in which diplomatic institutions were involved. Katarzyna Osos drew attention to a number of Poles who hold high positions: Donald Tusk, Mrs Bieńkowska and others. But I would like to say that these people owe these positions not to competitions, but to political decisions. They were directed either by the Polish authorities at that time, those who exercised power, either by the Civic Platform or as a result of agreements between states. These were political decisions, so it is difficult to compare these positions, where the substantive qualities of the candidate decide on winning the competition, with those where it is a political decision. The question asked by the president of Katarzyna Lubnauer was whether the current foreign policy of Poland was the beginning of polexit. Of course not. The answer is clear: membership in the European Union is crucial for Polish prosperity and for the security of Poland, for the position. I talked about it in my exposé. But this does not mean that we treat the European Union as an organisation without imperfection. We want it to change as we think it will be good for the Union and for our country. These national interests should be defended. This is not an egoistic approach, we want a better functioning Union that balances development differences between Member States. We want a competitive economic Union that is active in the neighbourhood. I would like to stress that we are demonstrating the need to increase the role of national parliaments in the decision-making process. This thread appeared repeatedly in the speech by Marcin Święcicki, in other speeches by representatives of the Platform. I would like to draw attention to a process that is taking place in the European Union. A few years ago, most of the political class in Poland was in favour of strengthening transnational institutions such as the European Commission. At that time, the paradigm dominated science that these institutions would defend the interests of the weaker countries in the long term. That is why we all in Poland thought, the whole political class, that these institutions should be supported. However, since around 2008, since the adoption of the Treaty of Lisbon and the financial crisis, we have seen the role of supranational institutions changing. There's a science dispute here, too. The second position, realistic, unlike the liberal one, is that supranational institutions do not defend the interests of these weaker countries, but they are an instrument for achieving the interests of the strongest countries. There's a consensus here that the realists have more arguments to support their thesis. When we look at the role of the European Commission during the financial crisis in Greece, where it was an instrument of implementation in the hands of others, or the role of the European Commission in relations with Hungary, or finally when it comes to Article. 7, in my opinion, the European Commission does not have an autonomous role here, but does not defend these weaker countries, but is simply to discipline them. This results in a change of orientation in foreign policy, including the Law and Justice, in support of these intergovernmental elements, such as the European Parliament, which give rise to legitimacy, because we ourselves experience that we are not treated like other countries. Double standards are applied. In other words, this evolution in relation to these different supranational or intergovernmental institutions is the result of the change in these institutions itself. Katarzyna Lubnauer asked how the government intends to stop EU officials from linking payments from the EU budget with compliance with the rule of law. On 8 February, I met Commissioner Věra Jourova, who is responsible for justice. We've been discussing this. I also presented our rations to other Member States. The European Commission has the right to make various proposals, but it will be up to the Member States to decide on this. There are no treaty grounds for applying such a link. The methods of spending European funds should be transparent. There is no justification for introducing vague solutions. How can we measure the compatibility of some judicial system with European Union law? The White Paper shows that the same solutions as those introduced by Poland have existed for many years, always in other countries of the European Union. I would like to say - this is the result of this document - that in the Polish system judges are more independent than in other countries, therefore it would be difficult to apply these criteria here. There are also institutions that control the spending of EU funds, such as OLAF, i.e. the European Office for Foreign Affairs. Anti-fraud. We support this work because we believe that transparency should be promoted in this way when it comes to spending EU funds. Madam President Lubnauer's question on the crisis in Poland - Israel, i.e. Why don't we have an ambassador? No. Minister Waszczykowski also spoke about this. Mr Jacek Khodorowicz has completed his mission since 2012. The mission is scheduled to end this year, but nothing has changed. The successor is emerging. In any case, the facility operates continuously and efficiently, and Mr Jacek Chodorowicz represents our interests in Tel Aviv. Mr Władysław Kosiniak-Kamysz asks if he claims that Poland has not secured its citizens in negotiations with Great Britain on brexit. No, the rights of Poles in the UK are fully secured. The government introduced a number of entries into the so-called. exit agreements to achieve this goal. Our vote was crucial in this regard. Poles retain access to the labour market, social services, education and health services. Their rights will also be protected by the Court of Justice of the European Union, which the United Kingdom has agreed to. Mr Grzegorz Długi asks what is the idea of fighting for the budget of the European Union for the government. Poland clearly sets out its position on the European Union budget within the European Union. We conduct intergovernmental consultations, including within the Visegrad Group. As I said in my exposé, we have similar interests. On 26 March, Commissioner of the European Union for the budget Günther Oettinger will come to Warsaw. He will meet in Parliament, including the building, at the joint meeting of the Commission: the European Union and Finance. I will also have the pleasure of meeting him in the building of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, we will discuss this. During the visit, we will present Poland's priorities on this issue supported by solid arguments. It should also be remembered that the regulation on the multiannual financial framework is adopted unanimously, so no solution can be accepted without the consent of Poland or any other country. Mr Szłapka from Modernzny asks for Poland's position on membership in the euro area. I would say that this is not an end in itself, although, of course, it is an extremely important decision. In any case, the opinion of economists is divided. I would like to point to the opinion of those who claim to be present that there would be a risk of disturbance to the economy. I would like to remind you that now the Member States of the European Union, which have not adopted the euro, are developing faster than those of the euro area. That's a certain indicator, too. There are opinions that Poland has been through the financial crisis relatively calmly because it has not adopted the euro and was able to adapt its economy using the possibility of establishing the exchange rate. Our goal is to increase the potential and competitiveness of Poland, to reach the level and even to exceed the level of strong economies. I think that's what we're doing. The relative level of income in Poland increases, the competitiveness of the Polish economy increases. The question now is whether the possible adoption of the euro would improve this competitiveness and the good state of the Polish economy or not. Mr Grzegorz Długi asks if we have a blockade for positions in the European Union. I've already told you in part about the competitions. There is no blockade for higher posts in the Union. This year, Poles took the position of director of security policy and conflict prevention at the headquarters of the European External Action Service, and the position was very important for Poland. In recent years, we have seen an increase in the employment of Poles in the EU institutions. In 2012, nearly 2500 people were employed in the Union's structures, with 3,300 in 2017. Of course, there are such opinions - they have empirical confirmation - that Poles take on average lower positions because they later joined the European Union. In time, they'll be promoted, we hope. We want as many of them as possible in the highest positions. This is a difficult process, there are competitions. As you can see now, the situation is improving, so we hope it will continue to improve. Mrs Krynicka asks what is the issue of implementing EU law in Poland against other Member States. A reasonable question, after all, is spoken in connection with the article. 7 that Poland does not respect values, European law. No, Poland is taking many steps to implement European Union law on a timely basis. The reports published by the European Commission twice a year show the state of transposition of European Union law in the Union. The Commission has established that the deficit limit is 1%. Poland looks good, it is in the middle of the list of countries. For example, in the summer report in 2016, eight countries had a worse result than Poland, and last year 13 countries had a worse result than Poland. I'm not going to exchange them. I have a list of these countries. In other words, Poland is about in the middle, like other European countries. As regards the implementation of European Union law into the Polish system of law - this raises the question of whether they were imposed on our country and how much financial penalties were imposed in connection with the timely implementation of European Union law against other countries - I would like to stress that Poland has never paid a penalty. There are currently 13 such cases before the Court, none against Poland. I would like to add that on 25 January the Commission decided to bring a complaint against Poland due to the delay in the implementation of the Directive on collective management of copyrights. As a sign of these actions, streets named after Marshal Piłsudski can still be found in Georgia. After the collapse of the Soviet Union, when Georgians could once again enjoy an independent state, we resumed our cooperation. Georgia is a very important member of the Eastern Partnership, which Poland is the main initiator and coordinator. Thanks to the establishment of the Parliamentary Assembly of the Republic of Poland and Georgia, we will be able to strengthen Poland's relations with Georgia as well as Georgia's relations with the European Union as a whole. ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u189	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Andrzej Noblet, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u190	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the government draft law amending the Payment Services Act and some other laws, print No 2225. As I stressed at the first reading of this draft law, its aim is to align Polish rules on the way in which payment services are provided with the requirements of Directive (EU) of the European Parliament and of the Council of 25 November 2015 called the PSD Directive2. This bill is a comprehensive amendment of the Payment Services Act of 19 August 2011. The European Commission, through the PSD2 Directive, extends the scope of the previous PSD to new services and payment service providers and extends the scope of existing services. These are payment instruments issued by payment service providers who do not maintain a payment service user account, which allows access to payment accounts. PSD2 amends the telecoms exemption by limiting it to a micro-payment for digital services and covers transactions with third countries when only one of the payment service providers is in the territory of the European Union. The PSD2 introduces improved security measures to be implemented by all payment service providers, including banks. This draft law, which implements the PSD2, aims to lay down rules that eliminate identified regulatory gaps so as to ensure greater legal transparency and consistent application of the legislative framework across the European Union. The Act is intended to provide equivalent conditions for action to promote new payment methods among a wide range of users and to ensure a high level of consumer protection when using these payment services throughout the European Union. This should improve the efficiency of the whole payment system and create a wider range of payment services and transparency while enhancing consumer confidence in the harmonised payment market. During a few hours of discussion, the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance discussed in detail the various articles of the Act in question with the participation of the Ministry's representative. 20 amendments were also tabled during the debate, as the rapporteur said. Some of them clarified certain provisions, some of which were legislative in nature and thus gave greater clarity to certain provisions in the articles. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supported all amendments at the level of work in the Committee on Public Finance and will vote in favour of the bill amending the Payment Services Act. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u198	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! This law is indeed the implementation of the European Union's PSD2 Directive into Polish law. The PSD2 Directive is the implementation into the legal system that will include the system for the operation of new services and new technologies that have emerged in the payment services market in recent years, including the integration of the European payment market. It is also a formal introduction to this market of new providers of these services, thus extending, improving the rules and opportunities to compete, but also making payments using new technologies safer, better safeguarding the interests of the customers who use them, and thus protecting consumers is one of the main objectives of this directive. It is, of course, about the fintechs mentioned here, which are becoming more and more popular on the Internet, and so far have not been covered by the system. Of course, yesterday's discussion in the committee essentially focused on a few points, apart from legislative amendments, some adopted, some others - no. Industry representatives have quite important and, I think, legitimate reservations about the fact that in the whole law, instead of the word "automation," the word "authorization" was adopted, which in the banking world means two completely different activities. Authentication is a situation where the customer uses the correct PIN code, and authorisation is consent to the transaction. The biggest discussion was that service providers were to bear the burden of proving that the payment transaction had been authorized and correctly recorded in the system, it is precisely the authorized or certified word. What does that mean? This means that we will expect banks and other financial service providers to prove to the bank in case of payment problems that the payment has been authorised, i.e. that the customer has agreed to the payment or use its login. This will in principle be difficult for the payment provider to prove and it seems appropriate to claim that, however, the idea of authentication should be used, so that the bank has received a properly signed transfer. Of course, you can argue, but in fact the security of your personal data, PIN, your account data should also belong to your client. It is certainly worth returning to this issue at the Senate stage, once again discussing, considering how, on the one hand, to safeguard the interests of customers using electronic payments, and on the other hand, to expect them to properly secure their data, not to transmit this data to third parties in an unauthorized manner when they do not want them to be used against them. There was also a mention of this... The entry of new services and new technologies into the market in the case of our country will be authorized by KNF. It has a deadline for this, there has been a discussion as to whether this deadline is sufficient. KNF expected that non-compliance within the deadline of consent would not automatically imply consent. The industry has said that when new technologies are introduced, time is of great importance and KNF has to deal with their proposals in due time. In fact, living in today's modern technology world, it is difficult not to agree that time plays a key role. Like I said, the Modern Club will support this bill because it's worth it. [[♪ The bell ♪]] new technologies in the system so that customers can use them more safely than they currently do. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u200	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I will present my position on the report of the Committee on Public Finances on the Government bill amending the Payment Services Act, i.e. prints No 2225 and 2368. The draft law was examined at meetings of the Permanent Sub-Committee on Financial Institutions and the Committee on Public Finance. I will remind you that the project concerns the implementation of EU law, i.e. the so-called directives. PSD2 of 25 November 2015 on payment services in the internal market. The project eliminates regulatory gaps in existing legislation, introduces new players in the payment services market, new technologies, harmonises operating conditions and normalizes the payment market across the European Union. The aim of the project is therefore to make cross-border payments in a way that is primarily safe for consumers in all Member States. The service directory has been extended by two types of services, i.e. about the payment initiation service and the information access service. Both services are merely ancillary, informational or checking service and do not directly concern the holding and transfer of funds. It also introduces a new entity, so-called. a small payment institution. This is a rather interesting subject. It can be run by small and medium-sized enterprises alongside other economic activities. These small payment institutions will only be able to carry out their activities in Poland once certain prudential requirements have been met, once they have been registered with the Financial Supervision Authority, and their turnover must not exceed EUR 1,5 million per month. In the course of the work of the subcommittee and the committee, a number of changes of an editorial, detailed and legislative nature have been introduced in the project, as well as substantive changes. Among the latter, i.e. substantive changes, are among others. those relating to the regulation of payment charges carried out by a telecommunications trader, the introduction of detailed rules for the Financial Supervision Commission in the event of a decision ordering the disposal of shares or shares of a national payment institution, as well as the possibility of imposing fines in the event of failure to order orders, the regulation of interpretation issues issued by the Financial Supervision Commission, the establishment of legal bases for making information covered by banking and professional secrecy available, the transmission by payment service providers of information necessary for the conduct of an online browser by KNF, and the exemption of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego from certain obligations arising from the Act and the introduction of references directly to the provisions of the delegated Regulation of the European Commission. I would also like to point out that, in the course of the work on the project, divergences have not been overcome, among others. with laws governing the principles of entrepreneurship, the Freedom of Business Act or the Anti-Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Act. The government side, i.e. the applicants, has committed themselves to regulating these issues at the stage of work in the Senate. In conclusion, it should be noted that the bill should meet the objective of ensuring better protection for payers, increasing consumer rights in turnover within the European Union and even payments in currencies outside the European Union. The Act will necessarily increase competition in the payment market and will generally foster the development of non-cash trading, new technologies and the further development of the information society. My club supports this project and will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much.

The purpose of this draft law is to amend the rules governing payment transactions made using an incorrect unique identifier. The proposed rules are intended to enable the effective recovery of the amounts of payment transactions carried out using incorrect payment account numbers which the payers have failed to recover as a result of their supplier's actions. The bill is a partial implementation into the Polish legal order in the field of art. 88 provisions of Directive 2009/138/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 25 November 2015 on payment services in the internal market, hereinafter referred to as the PSD Directive2. The purpose of the PSD2 Directive is to equip the payment service provider with the power to provide the payer with information enabling him to make a legal claim in order to recover the amount of the payment transaction executed using a erroneous unique identifier, where the payee does not voluntarily surrender that amount and to provide a legal basis for such action in cooperation with the payer's supplier with the payee. The President of the Republic of Poland, the financial spokesman, presented the proposal for the above amendments. ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u209	Thank you very much. Mrs Paulina Hennig-Kloska, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u210	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Gentlemen of Ministers! Minister! The purpose of the presidential bill amending the Payment Services Act, print No. 1606, is to enable citizens to recover the money transferred to the wrongly indicated recipient account effectively. Even some of us may have had to transfer money in our lives, not to the bill number we should have, or not to the recipient we should have. By October 2011, both banks and SKOKs carrying out the transfer ordered by the client were required to verify the correctness not only of the account number to which the payment is to be made but also of the name of the payee, i.e. the name of the payee, or the name of the company for which the payment is made. This allowed it to be verified whether the funds actually flowed to the relevant account belonging to the payee of the payment indicated in the transfer. According to those rules, when a bank or SKOK executed a transaction despite the non-compliance of these data, the client was able to effectively recover its funds from the entity that made the payment, that is, the bank or other financial institution. The situation changed in the end of 2011 following the entry into force of another Payment Services Act, which introduced the principle that a payment transaction can only be executed on the basis of an account number, regardless of any other data given to the payee, including the name, name or company name. This law was the implementation of EU law into Polish law. However, it seems that the overzealousness of the Polish legislator, rather than the literally read EU directive itself, led to the situation that the transferors today do not have to verify whether the bill actually indicated in the transfer order belongs to the person, the company indicated in the order as the recipient. In the light of the law in force, the effectiveness of the recovery of money today depends essentially on the will of the person or company to whom the money will be transferred wrongly. The bank can only apply to the payee for a refund of the wrong amount, and the one he sent can only count on the good will of the recipient. The presidential proposal envisages two changes to improve the situation of such distracted payers. Firstly, it is intended to protect the personal data of persons who decide to voluntarily return the funds so that their sensitive data are not visible when the funds are returned. Secondly, it introduces an exception to the obligation of banking secrecy where the recipient of the transfer does not wish to pay voluntarily the funds that have affected his account and were not due to him so that the person ordering the transfer could already bring an action to the court in order to recover his funds. The thing seems natural. In order to claim their misplaced money, the payers need to know who to sue for reimbursement, who to turn to. In this respect, the exemption from this banking secret is worth considering, probably justified. It is important to enable citizens to investigate misplaced funds, but I have the impression that a slightly different solution could have been proposed that would have solved the problem more effectively. The point is that we could delimit those payments where there is no designated recipient. Then, in fact, the bank would have to accept the payment and could have made the transfer in spite of the lack of data, i.e. if it had no customer at all, only the account number. The name, the name or the name of the company to which the transfer is directed, but at the moment... we could leave this verification. It seems to me that the directive implemented in 2011 left such a freedom to national legislation that it could be left in a situation where the data are entered into the transfer, but in fact the bank would wrongly verify and reject the transfer. Otherwise, we expose ourselves to unnecessary claims, unnecessary legal actions. [[♪ The bell ♪]] All of this could have been avoided if the Directive had been properly implemented in 2011. Today, in the Senate, it is worth to bend over to consider whether or not this solution is ultimately implemented in a different way. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u211	Thank you very much, Member. Ms Genowefa Tokarska, the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u212	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Committee on Public Finances on the presidential bill amending the Act on Payment Services, Print No. 1606 and 2369. The draft law was examined at the first reading at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance, then at the Permanent Sub-Committee on Financial Institutions and again at the Committee on Public Finance. Several changes of an editorial and order nature were introduced during the work. The draft law generally concerns the partial implementation of the PSD2 Directive and is a complement to the government's proposal for a comprehensive alignment with EU law, on which we have just concluded the debate. The draft law amends the rules on payment transactions executed using an incorrect unique identifier. They are intended to enable the effective recovery of the amounts of transactions carried out using incorrect payment account numbers which the payers have failed to recover as a result of their supplier's actions. According to Art. A payment order shall be deemed to have been executed to the payee if it has been executed in accordance with a unique identifier, i.e. the customer's account number. If the unique identifier provided by the customer is incorrect, the supplier shall not be liable for failure to comply with or inappropriate execution of the payment transaction. Although the customer is still entitled to an investigation of his claims or claims by court, personal data of the payee of the transfer are required to file a legal action which, in the current legal state, cannot be disclosed or made available. The PSD2 Directive removes these inconveniences and concludes that, where it is not possible to collect cash, the payer's payment service provider shall provide the payer, upon written request, with all the information available to the payer's payment service provider relevant for him to make a legal claim to recover the money. The Presidential bill introduces solutions that are currently foreseen in the PSD2 Directive, and to this end payment service providers will be required to operate an interest-free, technical so-called. a refund account - a special purpose account only for the purpose of returning funds to the payer. The reimbursement will not be made directly to the payer's payment account or to the payer's supplier's account, which will ensure the protection of personal data. The bill assumes a 3-month period of vacathio legis. This will enable the institutions to prepare for the application of the proposed solutions. My club, the Members' Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, welcomes this project and will vote in favour of its adoption. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u239	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u240	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft amendment of the law improves the functioning of the telecommunications market and clarifies the provisions relating to its participants. It also includes solutions to improve the operation of the telecoms regulator - the President of the Office of Electronic Communications. One of the most important changes is the introduction of a new system for alerting citizens to threats that could cause a crisis in a given area. It will allow you to send short text messages to all subscribers in a specific area with a threat warning. This information is to be prepared by the Government Security Center. This will significantly improve the safety of Polish residents. The RCB will be able to benefit from the immediate and free assistance of all network operators with subscribers on the site. But it's not the only change. Others include increased protection against telecommunications abuse using high-charge numbers. In order to improve safety, operators or companies using their services will be subject to new obligations, in particular information, in order to clearly inform the customer of the service providers of the increased fee and the suppliers of the additional service. This also involves the commissioning of additional tools for the blockhouse of unwanted services with high charges. The Act will also serve to organise suppliers: it will also prohibit the provision of services on the market with an increased fee not included in the register of services maintained by the President of the Office for Electronic Communications. With the new rules, local authorities will have easier access to services supporting the distribution of free Internet, possibly lower prices than the market price. Finally, it is worth stressing the introduction by the law of a new reserved form of contract for the provision of publicly available telecommunications services, provided for by the provisions of the revised Civil Code - a document form - that means that the contract for the provision of telecommunications services will now be possible in writing - e.g. in the customer service office, and also remotely - e.g. by SMS, e-mail, telephone or qualified e-signatures. This will all contribute significantly to the development of the digital society in our country. The PSL Club will support the solutions introduced by the novel, which in our opinion are a consequence of civilization development. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u259	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending the Act on Social Insurance for Farmers and some other laws results from the implementation of the Seasonal Directive through the introduction of the Act on Employment Promotion, as well as from the fact of far-reaching consultations with the social side, which raised the issue of employment of workers on farms dealing with sadification and gardening. We ourselves have experience in employing such workers and, unfortunately, we need to assess them very critically. The point is, when she was doing a fictional job employing workers in the form of a work contract, those workers didn't have any form of security. At that time, the social side argued that, through the implementation of the Employment Promotion Act and the Seasonal Directive, it should regulate the cases of persons performing the task of a helper in harvesting, in the selection of fruits, vegetables, in order simply to provide security for these workers and to establish this within the framework of the law. Therefore, after consultation of the social side, the government presented a project which creates a new form of civil law agreement between the owner of the fruit holding or gardening and the person who will carry out these tasks on his farm. It's so-called. the helper's contract at harvest and this person will be included in the social security system, a separate system for farmers, i.e. the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund. The farmer will pay a contribution from such a person - this year - 174 zł consisting of two drones, i.e. from the so-called contribution. health and contributions so called accident. If, God forbid, something happened to such a person doing the work of a helper at harvest, it will receive a one-off accident benefit, as well as a health insurance insurance, which means he will also be able to benefit from medical insurance. This is a strong response to the expectations of the trade union organisations that deal with the agri-food sector, the fruit industry and the gardening industry. Those doing this work will themselves pay income tax on the basis of PIT-8C. The farmer will only be obliged by the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund to report this worker to the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund within 7 days of the start of work on the farm and to withdraw the contribution from it. In this law we also regulate other quite important things. The first important issue is the implementation of the recommendations of the Supreme Audit Chamber. This applies to the contribution fund. The activities and support of the organisations were based on the statute of the contribution fund, and from now on this issue will be regulated by the law, i.e. the overarching act. Another thing is already much less important and it also concerns the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund, it is the advice of this institution. The Council was composed of representatives of agricultural associations and organisations, and from now on, according to this law, agricultural self-government, i.e. the Agricultural Chamber, will also be included there, and representatives of the Agricultural Chamber will be able to be elected to the board of the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the fact that the project is very expected by the social side, we are asking for urgent proceedings over it. We want this law to enter into force at the beginning of the season, i.e. with the start of work on farms dealing with sadification and gardening, and we want helpers to have these basic social protections, so that there are no such cases as the recent case from Wielkopolska, or cases from Mazowsze and other regions of our country, where the helpers doing this work, the workers from these farms were left without such protection in really extreme conditions. It is a government party project, developed together with organisations. I'm calling for urgent investigation of this project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u307	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! 26 March 2018 will mark the anniversary of the life of the Polish astronomer, who surprised the world. His life and passion have been staged. It also has a monument near the house from which the roof watched the celestial spheres by handmade astronomical instruments. In the city with which he tied his fate, his name was called school and communication roundabout. This outstanding and unique figure is not Nicolaus Copernicus, although many amazing similarities can be found in his life's journey to the stars: scientific curiosity and passion combined with humility towards the Creator. This figure is Adam Giedrys, a resident of Szczecin in the Central Pomerania. It's an unusual character. Tailor by profession, astronomer by appointment. He was born in Vilnius on 26 March 1918, on the threshold of Polish independence. He became a master of tailoring and practiced his profession in Nowy Dwor near Ponowież, from which came outstanding engineer and constructor Stanisław Kierbedź, the creator of the famous bridge in Warsaw, named after his name by the Kierbedz bridge. During the war he belonged to the National Army - Vilnius District. After the war, in 1946, he settled in Szczecin. In 1948, he was arrested for anti-communist activities and was imprisoned in Wronki. That's where he got really sick. He was hospitalized in Olsztyn, where events took place, which later changed his life. He saw a book about astronomy, started reading it, and he couldn't stop. Astronomy absorbed him completely and filled his life. From the glasses, he built a telescope to observe the sky. On the roof of the building in which he lived, he built with the help of the authorities and people of goodwill an observation dome. He didn't keep his astronomical knowledge to himself. He quickly discovered the pedagogical talent and the need to share the secrets of heaven with the young generation. He took school trips, organized readings and seminars. He did not limit his work to Szczecink, but participated in international conventions, conducted correspondence with scientific centers and even with astronauts. He has prepared many who are infected with the astronomical passion of successors. Its uniqueness was noticed by NASA - an agency of the US government dealing with astronauts and space exploration. In recognition of his admirable passion, he was the first Pole to receive from NASA a sample of lunar land from Apollo 11 of 1969, the first landing of a man on the Moon, which was presented only in Szczecin in 1971. Adam Giedrys died in Szczecin on 26 December 1997. High Izbo! The legend of Adam Giedrys is already part of the history of the Central Pomerania - the story that people came from different parts of Poland after the war, discovering here a new, unknown world. They won him with passion, ingenuity, courage, dedication and patriotism. In the 100th anniversary of the birth of Poland reborn, on the 100th anniversary of the birth of Adam Giedrys, we pay tribute to him and to the generation of explorers who rebuilt, developed and integrated this new, unknown world with Poland. Thank you. [[Applause]]
