ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-21-sejm-60-2.u309	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Every year, on March 21 in Poland and in the world, the world day of people with Down syndrome is celebrated. This date was not chosen accidentally. It symbolizes the third chromosome of the 21st pair whose presence is the genetic cause of Down syndrome. In 1862 the English physician John Down first described Down's syndrome as a separate form of mental delay. In 1959, the French geneticist and pediatrician Jérôme Lejeune discovered the cause of this disorder called the 21st chromosome trisomy. The discovery of the relationship between intellectual disability and chromosome anomaly made it possible to reject the theory of the hereditary character of the team. It is worth mentioning here that in 2011 the American Journal of Medical Genetics published a report, the results of which show that 99% People with Down syndrome say they're happy. In addition, 79% parents said that their life prospects were more positive thanks to their children with Down syndrome and 88% My siblings feel like they're better people because of their siblings with Down syndrome. This year's celebration is under the slogan: what do I give my society? It aims to promote the rights of people with Down syndrome to participate fully in society. In many cities various actions are carried out as a sign of solidarity with people with Down's syndrome, such as the action of putting colored socks on this day not to pair. High Izbo! There's a reason I'm reminding you of this special celebration. It is important to me for personal reasons. I'm the mom of an adult woman with Down syndrome. With full awareness, I stress here that Magda, because her name is my daughter, is the great joy and strength of my life. Her appearance in this world has changed my life for the better. I have a special daughter who sees the world a little differently. This world is simple and beautiful, and people in it are good, they help each other, they smile at each other. Magda's the one who's still teaching me how to enjoy the little things. He has incredible confidence and serenity. He can sense my emotions, he can comfort me, and he's a very helpful person. I assure you that she is happy, independent and surrounded by a family that loves and supports her very much. Thanks to it, I experience what true unconditional and limitless love is. Thanks to it, I notice how much diversity is around us. I notice the need for tolerance and concern for such persons. I also personally know many happy families raising children with Down syndrome. What's the recipe for happiness? It is simple: first of all, you have to accept the child as he is, with his deficits and at the same time the huge potential that he has, and not let it be wasted. The danger to parents' happiness is their own selfishness and comfort. I'm sure it's not disabled children. Therefore, on this day I appeal to those of you who do not understand that we are happy having a disabled child. It is true that there are more problems, more work, more time we need to devote to rehabilitation, science, but we see the effects of our actions. We don't expect compassion and mercy. Contrary to popular opinion, we do not feel hurt by fate. We do not repent for our sins, and a disabled child is not a punishment for us. I also appeal to parents who are expecting a child with Down's syndrome: let him live, and it will cause true love to enter your lives, and many times you will feel the most beautiful joy and sincere pride. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Professor Jan Bernard Szlagi. The parish church played a very important role in my town. This oldest city church is primarily a place of religious, cultural and socio-political worship for the whole Chojnice region. The monumental walls of the Gothic temple dominate Chojnice. This is where the next generations of Chojniczan prayed for centuries. The church was consecrated for 20 years by Bishop Peter of Gniezno in 1365. The church was built in the style of Gothic Vistula. War riots, fires, the church's takeover by Protestants, during which the priest of that time was murdered. This event, as well as many other joyful and uplifting events, consists of almost 7 centuries of church history. The last fire in February 1945, after the Russians had entered and the war was completed, was completely consumed by the Chojnica fara. The city center was set on fire by soldiers of the victorious army. It took ten years to rebuild the temple. Chojnicki was visited and prayed by the Primate of Poland cardinals. Stefan Wyszyński, Primate of the Millennium, who visited Chojnice in 1953, shortly before the internment by the communist authorities of Poland in the framework of repression against the Catholic Church. In the 1980s, the activists of the NSZZ.Solidarity had great support from the parish priest Roman Lewandowski, who lent rooms for meetings, supported families of persecuted and interned activists.
